# bb2dash — Decision Log

Running log of design decisions and their reasoning. Append-only; newest last.

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-02 | Grading stored as declarative rules, not weight columns | 6 courses use 4 structurally different grading models |
| 2026-09-02 | Facts (`assignments`) separated from state (`assignment_progress`) | re-sync can rewrite facts without touching Stack's planner |
| 2026-09-02 | Every fact row carries `source` + `confidence` | reconciliation only overwrites `tentative`/`inferred`; conflicts with `confirmed` get surfaced |
| 2026-09-03 | Crawler holds only the publishable key; insert-only RLS | browser-side code must never see the service key |
| 2026-09-09 | Embeddings in their own table keyed `(text_id, model, part_no)`, not a column on `bb_file_text` | re-embedding with a new model never touches extraction data; long units split without re-chunking |
| 2026-09-09 | Embedding model: **gte-small via Supabase Edge Functions** (384-dim, OSS, $0) | solves query-time embedding with the same free code path; no Docker, no API cost; upgrade = re-embed ~1.2k rows |
| 2026-09-09 | Corpus chunks embedded with `"{course} {bucket} — {file_name}: "` header; queries embedded raw | header carries context for thin slides; prefixing the query would push it away from passages |
| 2026-09-09 | `embed-corpus` persists per-part, not per-unit | edge CPU budget kills long units mid-request; per-part insert makes kills lossless and re-runs idempotent |
| 2026-09-09 | Hub retrieval default: **hybrid** (RRF) | ties vector on ranking (hit@1 9/10) but returns 10 distinct units per 10 results vs ~6; degrades to vector when FTS finds nothing |
| 2026-09-09 | `pg_net` enabled and kept | only route from sandboxed sessions to edge functions (org egress blocks supabase.co); pairs with pg_cron later |
| 2026-09-09 | Docker: skipped | Postgres+pgvector managed by Supabase; model lives in edge runtime; nothing to containerize |
| 2026-09-09 | Workflow SOP: dev on branches, push per completed task, **one PR per phase**, merge only on Stack's word | Stack's instruction |
| 2026-09-09 | GUI stack: **Next.js on Vercel + Supabase Auth**, no Docker | supersedes the recovered local "Docker-first hub" plan; pg_cron+pg_net cover the scheduler jobs; see `docs/planning/sprint-0-foundation/40_RECONCILIATION_2026-09-09.md` |
| 2026-09-09 | Electron endgame parked to a later phase, not dropped | its only unique value (open local OneDrive files, spawn `claude`) doesn't survive on Vercel; browser fallbacks (signed URLs, copy-path) ship first |
| 2026-09-09 | Renderer: CSS Modules + custom properties, **no Tailwind** | the `.dc.html` Nocturne artboards are the layout spec and port 1:1 |
| 2026-09-09 | GUI v1 scope cuts: no bell/announcements, no planner day view, **no grade display** this term, 14-day tracker window | honesty rule (no gradebook data yet) + reconciliation scope |
| 2026-09-09 | Effort model from `20_D1` §3 (19-type base, ≥5-points multiplier gate, source label per figure) | authoritative over the `21_D2` variant where they disagreed |
| 2026-09-09 | Screen workers run in **isolated git worktrees**, PM integrates by copying deliverable files | prevents 4 parallel Opus workers (and a concurrent external session) from clobbering one tree |
| 2026-09-09 | Adopted the out-of-band `hybrid_search_file_text` upgrade (relevance floor + similarity), captured as migration 018 | backward compatible (null-default param); a concurrent session applied it under a colliding `012` name; reverting good work made no sense |
| 2026-09-09 | RLS hardening (W-9) sequenced AFTER the first live preview | a policy bug during hardening must not be confused with a screen bug; also safer to tighten once, late, while a second session writes prod |
| 2026-09-09 | A second Claude session also edits bb2dash (prod + repo) | fetch-before-act, never force-push, flag anything not originated here — treat prod and branch as shared |
| 2026-09-10 | `main`'s `012_hybrid_similarity` + `013_hybrid_similarity_single_source` are the **canonical** hybrid-similarity migrations; gui-v1's `018_hybrid_similarity.sql` deleted | 018 was a stale copy of main's 012 (main's 013 further refined the function; prod holds 013); one source of truth per migration |
| 2026-09-10 | GUI v1 migrations renumbered 012–017 → **014–019** (`planner_columns`, `effort_base`, `work_items`, `course_display`, `files_current`, `sync_contract`) after merging main into `feat/gui-v1` | two sessions took 012+ concurrently; the GUI set is independent of `hybrid_search_file_text`, so slotting it after 013 yields the same final schema; `git mv` keeps history |
| 2026-09-10 | The renumber is **repo-only**; prod `schema_migrations` keeps the old names (012–017) for the same DDL — accepted name-level artifact, not drift. Never re-apply 014–019 | the objects are live once; a re-apply would fail or double-record; a rebuild in README order reproduces prod. See `docs/planning/sprint-0-foundation/41_RECONCILIATION_gui_vs_retrieval-mcp.md` |
| 2026-09-10 | W-8 palette does NOT send `min_similarity` to search v3; it keeps labelling sub-0.80 hits "keyword match" | the overlay prefers "always show something" (v3 header says as much); the floor is for agent callers (mcp-server default 0.78) |
| 2026-09-10 | RLS hardened to owner-scoped (migration 020): 21 `using(true)` authenticated policies → `auth.uid() = public.app_owner()`; storage `bb_files_auth_all` ANDed with the owner check; anon INSERT paths + service_role bypass preserved | closes W-9; only the sole owner's JWT can read/write. Verified: owner sees all rows, any other authenticated uid sees zero, anon insert + `search` still work |
| 2026-09-10 | `public.app_owner()` resolves the owner **by email** (`emstacho@syr.edu`) via a SECURITY DEFINER lookup on `auth.users`, NOT a hardcoded uid | the account was recreated once already (uid changed `e2c3c645…`→`fd0b7c9d…`); a literal uid would silently lock the sole user out on the next recreation. Email-based survives it with no migration. A hardcoded-uid draft was written and rejected. Guard: migration aborts if the email yields no user. Trade-off: a new advisor WARN (authenticated can call the SECURITY DEFINER `app_owner` via RPC) — accepted; the grant is required for policy evaluation and the function only returns the owner's own uid |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 7 workers run on their own branches (`feat/<phase>-db`, `feat/<phase>-clients`) in separate worktrees, cut from the phase branch; the PM merges them back | refines the 2026-09-09 worktree decision: git refuses one branch in two worktrees, and per-worker branches give each worker its own push target and a clean merge instead of file copying |
| 2026-09-10 | Hybrid `snippet` is the **matched passage**, plain text: `ts_headline` with `StartSel`/`StopSel` emptied over the best embedding part's slice for FTS-arm hits, that slice's head for vector-only hits; `snippet_source` says which | the head of a 16-part syllabus answered every question with the instructor's phone number; `part_range` was stored and unused. No markup because both clients (React palette, MCP markdown) would have to escape it again; highlighting is a client concern |
| 2026-09-10 | Snippet length governed by a word budget (`MaxFragments=2, MaxWords=40, MinWords=12`), not the contract's "≤ ~400 chars"; observed 81–791 chars | ts_headline has no char cap; the frozen option string was kept and clients truncate for display. Revisit only if a client needs a hard cap |
| 2026-09-10 | Superseded files are dropped **before ranking** in all three search RPCs (`p_include_superseded boolean default false`); `search` v4 / MCP / web expose `include_superseded`, sent only when true | one current document is the safe default; stale drafts must not consume rank positions either. Sent-only-when-true for the same PGRST202 reason as `p_min_similarity` |
| 2026-09-10 | `superseded_by` seeded by data migration 022 for exactly the four stale IST.466 rows 018 named, matched by **sha256** and justified from `bb_files.notes` + `bb_raw`; 16 vs 40 deliberately NOT ordered (two locations of the same Sep 3 document — both point at 66) | the bb-classify-files pass that 018 expected never ran; ids are generated identities, content hashes are stable; an unprovable ordering would be a fabricated fact. Other near-duplicates (17, 18/19, 31/32/47) left for a general rule |
| 2026-09-10 | `part_range` is defined in **code points** (`Array.from(text)`), matching Postgres `char_length`/`substring`; `embed-corpus` v5 chunks that way, 023 clamps the one historical overrun instead of re-embedding | JS UTF-16 `.length` counted the emoji on text 276 as two chars; the parity harness shows identical part lists on BMP text and a prod dry-run reports 0 missing parts, so a re-embed would change nothing |
| 2026-09-10 | `web/` test harness is vitest + Testing Library (jsdom), versions pinned exact; coverage `include` scoped to `queries.search.ts` until screens gain tests | the app had zero tests; the palette shell needs a router + query client to render, and a 47% headline that counts untested screens describes nothing |
| 2026-09-10 | Keyword-arm snippets are cut from a part whose slice **covers the tsquery** — the highest-`ts_rank` covering part, vector-best part as tiebreak, lowest `part_no` last — else `ts_headline` over the whole unit; `part_no` = the part the snippet was cut from (null for the fallback) (migrations 024–025) | 021 used the vector-best part for every row, and `/code-review` showed on prod that half the "matched passage" rows for `attendance policy` did not contain the keyword — the old head-of-unit bug with confident, wrong provenance. Lowest-covering-part (024 alone) was deterministic but picked part 8 over the actual "Final Exam Day" line in part 16; `ts_rank` picks 16 outright |
| 2026-09-10 | Accepted +25% query time (≈22 → 27 ms, limit 12) for the ranked cover rule; the stored per-part `tsvector` that would remove it is backlog, not this phase | the cost is CPU in `to_tsvector`/`ts_rank` per covering part, buffers barely moved; well under the ~60 ms ceiling set for the palette; a schema change nobody asked for does not belong in a fix round |
| 2026-09-10 | `[notes]` handling for sliced snippets is server-side: prefix `[notes] ` when the marker precedes the slice; whole-unit fallback headlines only the text before the marker (024) | CLAUDE.md's "search UIs must scrub/label speaker notes" is a hard rule; both client scrubbers locate the marker inside the snippet, so a slice after the marker would have shown notes unlabeled. Re-using the marker keeps both clients unchanged. Latent today (no multi-part `[notes]` unit) — fixed in the same migration family that created the gap |
| 2026-09-10 | Keyword-mode (`search_file_text`) headline is plain text too (`StartSel`/`StopSel` emptied, 024); `limit` applied inside `fused` before the joins | the palette exposes Keyword mode and rendered literal `<b>` tags; ordering is fully determined by `(score, text_id)`, so limiting earlier is free and halves the joined rows |
| 2026-09-10 | MCP excerpt label depends on **mode** first, `snippet_source` second; web `similarity` is `number \| null` and null means "keyword match" | only hybrid returns `snippet_source`, so fts/vector rows were mislabelled "unit head"; a unit extracted but not yet embedded has null similarity and can only be a keyword hit — it must wear the badge, not vanish between the two labels |
| 2026-09-10 | Post-Phase 7 scope is `docs/planning/sprint-1-hub/60_REQUIREMENTS_v2.md`; course page is **Google Classroom-style** (Stream landing, Classwork by Blackboard folder, Grades, Info), timeline kept as a sub-view | Stack's direction after five clarification rounds; supersedes the T-list, D1 ranks, Sep 8 phases and the STATUS backlog |
| 2026-09-10 | Parallel phases get **non-overlapping migration ranges** (Phase 8 = 026–029, Phase 9 = 030–039) allocated in the briefs | two sessions taking 012+ concurrently forced a renumber once; a reserved range costs nothing |
| 2026-09-10 | Phase 8 owns `stage_content(uuid)`; Phase 9 owns the driver and every other stage and calls it, skipping with a recorded stage row if absent | one seam between two parallel phases, frozen in both briefs |
| 2026-09-10 | Course resolution from `bb_raw` uses `courses.bb_id` first, `bb_course_id` as fallback | `bb_raw.bb_course_id` holds Blackboard's internal id (`_569316_1`); the brief's rule matched 0 of 7 shells |
| 2026-09-10 | New views are created `security_invoker` with anon revoked; the 001–025 views (owner-executed, RLS bypass) get one fix-up migration in Phase 9 | `set role anon; select count(*) from v_work_items` returned all 152 rows despite migration 020; tables were hardened, views were not |
| 2026-09-10 | `courses.card_note` stored verbatim, capped at 280 by check constraint, rendered as text only | owner-typed; sanitising would rewrite what he wrote; React escapes on render |
| 2026-09-10 | Hand-narrowed row interfaces kept for `v_course_stream` / `v_content_tree` / `v_course_display` after regenerating `database.types.ts` | generated view types are all-nullable; the narrow interfaces are the contract and are asserted at the call site |
| 2026-09-10 | Tracker fetches 56 days, shows 14 with ◂ ▸ paging; the Home course card's "Next due" stays bounded to 14 days | reinstates the artboard's 8-week horizon without silently changing what the card claims |
| 2026-09-10 | Popouts are route-driven (`?item=assignment:<id>` / `session:<id>`), mounted once in the app layout | any screen can open one; Phase 8 tabs are routes so the query string is free |
| 2026-09-10 | IST.466's duplicate content paths left as first-wins (5 rows counted, none invented) | a disambiguator would change `path` and break the tree contract; a key change belongs to a later phase |
| 2026-09-10 | AI policy is **display only**; no enforcement in the MCP server or skills. Professional-side stub **dropped** | Stack's calls in the clarification rounds |
| 2026-09-13 | One `FileOpenAction` component owns the file Open ladder on every screen; `fileHonesty` answers `unknown` ("Not stored") for fields a source does not carry rather than asserting there is no route | four hand-copied ladders had already drifted in label and style (Phase 8 code review) |
| 2026-09-14 | GUI decision 1c amended: ☰ toggles a **course sidebar**, not a pop-down. The rail holds the course list; `.main` drops its `--content-max` cap and fills the rest of the width | Stack saw the deployed preview: the capped column was never centred, so a wide window hugged the left edge and left dead space on the right. Centring it would have widened the gap on both sides; the rail turns the space into navigation. In flow at ≥1024px, an overlay drawer below it |
| 2026-09-14 | The sidebar's side is one token, `--sidebar-side` in globals.css, read as a flex-direction (`row` = left, `row-reverse` = right) by the shell row, the drawer wrapper and the rail's hairline | right (Stack, 2026-09-14) (Google Classroom's class list), but Stack asked that it be flippable. Expressing it as a flex-direction rather than an inset keeps all three call sites on the same CSS property, so one value moves them together |
| 2026-09-14 | Sidebar open/closed lives in `html[data-sidebar]`, stamped by an inline boot script before first paint and owned by React after mount; the choice persists in `localStorage['bb2dash.sidebar']` with the viewport default (open ≥1024px) as the fallback | driving the collapse from a class name would have shown the server's default for a frame on every reload. The attribute is written during HTML parse, so the rail and the ☰ highlight are correct on frame one, and React never renders the attribute — there is no hydration mismatch to suppress |
| 2026-09-10 | `bb-files` Storage bucket **private** (migration 030); reads go through signed URLs only | professors' files were fetchable anonymously by derivable path since 2026-09-02; the web app already minted signed URLs |
| 2026-09-10 | Transform runs as SQL functions on **pg_cron inside Postgres** (`transform_tick` every 2 min), not as an edge function or a local hub | nothing leaves Supabase; sandbox egress never applies; pg_cron enabled for it |
| 2026-09-10 | The transform folds **only crawls registered on an owner-claimed `agent_requests` row** (`agent_requests.run_id`, migration 039); unregistered `bb_raw` runs are quarantined once, never folded; `bb_raw` unique on `(run_id, kind, bb_course_id)` | `bb_raw` is anon-insertable by design; without the guard two anonymous POSTs would have put attacker-chosen content on the owner's dashboard (Phase 8 security review) |
| 2026-09-10 | **All 15 public views are `security_invoker`** with anon revoked (migration 036, guard block refuses a future non-invoker view) | views created before 020 ran as owner and bypassed RLS; `v_work_items` returned 159 rows to anon |
| 2026-09-10 | Inbox resolutions carry a free-text `resolution_note` ("why") and are applied by the next transform, never directly; shapes: `conflict` `{accept}`, `stack_must_confirm`/`missing` `{value, value_type}`, `deadline`/`data_gap` `{dismissed}` | Stack wants the reasons kept for tuning the rules; the transform stays the only writer of typed facts |
| 2026-09-10 | Sync cadence: **Stack triggers**, the app does the rest; no scheduled crawl, no reminders | Duo; Stack's call. The Sync button enqueues, the `bb-sync` skill crawls inside a logged-in tab |
| 2026-09-11 | Only the newest crawl may declare a file missing (`stage_files` replay guard, 037) | replaying an older crawl after a newer one stamped 22 present files missing |
| 2026-09-11 | SECURITY DEFINER transform functions are not callable by `authenticated` (038); the app's only path to a transform is an `agent_requests` row | advisor finding; the RPC surface bought nothing |
| 2026-09-13 | `v_data_freshness` ignores `status = 'skipped'` rows and unregistered runs (migration 040; PM extended Phase 9's range by one number for it) | the Phase 9 security review showed two anonymous `bb_raw` POSTs could pin "crawl last attempt failed" on Home forever via the quarantine bookkeeping row; ical's daily skipped row did the same legitimately |
| 2026-09-13 | **Merge order: Phase 8 (PR course-dimension) before Phase 9 (PR sync-loop)**; Phase 9's post-review fixes take migrations 041–045 | 036's guard requires every public view to be security_invoker, and `v_course_display`'s flag comes from Phase 8's 028; replaying main with 030+ before 026–029 would abort at 036. Prod already holds both, so this is a repo-order rule, not drift |
| 2026-09-14 | `attention_items` dedupes only while `state = 'open'` (041); a "Keep mine" answer stands until Blackboard's value changes (042); `applied_at` is set only when a fact was written, otherwise the row stays "answered, applies on next sync" (042) | Phase 9 code review: the full-key unique index blocked the second resolution of any recurring question, and re-raising a kept conflict every sync made the Inbox unclearable |
| 2026-09-14 | iCal responses are collected by `ical_collect()` on every 2-minute tick, not by the daily poll (044) | pg_net drops `net._http_response` rows after 6 h; a daily two-phase poll could never read its own response |
| 2026-09-15 | One open `sync` request at a time: while a `queued`/`claimed` sync exists, the Sync button re-copies its command instead of inserting another row | the tick never touches `kind = 'sync'` (those need a browser), so a double press on 2026-09-14 left request 9 queued with nothing to close it; the skill only ever claims one |
| 2026-09-15 | Date-only `due_date` values are formatted as the date they are, never via `::timestamptz at time zone` (045) | the cast is midnight UTC, and New York is the previous evening, so conflict questions showed bb2dash's date one day early; only text was wrong, the applied value took a different path |
| 2026-09-14 | **Definition of done for every remaining phase = SOP gates + Stack's acceptance script** walked on the Vercel preview; sign-off once per phase, at the PR | Stack's answer in the MVP/DoD questionnaire (`docs/planning/sprint-1-hub/70_MVP_INDEX.md` §1.1); per-milestone sign-off was offered and declined |
| 2026-09-14 | **Every task carries an executable check** (test, SQL assertion, curl, screenshot diff) the worker runs itself, plus one demo line for the acceptance script; a task without a check is not a task | Stack's answer; it is what makes the task loops (check → build → verify → repeat) honest rather than a to-do list |
| 2026-09-14 | Phase 10 split: **10a** = gradebook mirror, Grades page + tab with latest scores, submission pull-back with files, web-only upload; **10b** = model, what-if (entry + target solver), one saved scenario per course, score history; courses with non-computable methods show only Blackboard's number | 10b is time-gated on real scores and on V-1; a course with no Blackboard total shows "publishes no total" until V-1's questions step says otherwise |
| 2026-09-14 | Google Calendar push is **due dates only** on one dedicated calendar; meetings are not pushed (supersedes Requirements v2 §6.2 #1) | Stack's choice; fewer events, no recurrence logic, API push (not an ICS feed, which Google refreshes only every 12–24 h) |
| 2026-09-14 | Bell: **opening the dropdown marks its items seen** (badge clears); no per-item read tracking in the MVP | Stack's choice; simplest honest semantics |
| 2026-09-14 | Electron MVP = shell (own window, taskbar icon, single instance) + desktop notifications for all three R-26 triggers + Sync button; **the OneDrive mirror is a post-MVP task** in the same phase; notifications come from a main-process **poller** with a watermark, not Realtime | Stack's choice on scope; research: background events belong to polling (sockets die across sleep), and "due tomorrow" is a clock trigger with no DB write behind it |
| 2026-09-14 | V-1 gains a **per-course questions block** (no-total courses, OCR-only files, week/session classification, ambiguous aggregations) answered by Stack inline; rows are decided from the materials, Stack may ask the professor and override; done = all seven courses signed off; verdict files carry a YAML machine block so a later automated pass can re-check | Stack's answers; research: Great Expectations' suite-as-data + docs-as-prose split, and accounting reconciliation's preparer/reviewer roles |
| 2026-09-14 | V-2: hook auto-tags with manual additions allowed (hook merges frontmatter), existing notes moved and back-filled from git, a session is concluded after 24 h without a resume; DoD = the brief's acceptance list, PM-verified | Stack's answers; research: ADR-style status ratchet (never regress; a post-sweep resume starts a new note) and pgvector's need for a GIN index because HNSW has no in-index filtering |
| 2026-09-14 | Styling direction is decided **after functionality is achieved**; Phase 13's DoD is fixed now (every screen on tokens, light + dark, Stack approves each) | Stack's answer; the DoD can be written without the direction, the direction cannot be chosen without the screens |
| 2026-09-14 | Research reports for each phase live in `docs/planning/sprint-1-hub/research/` and are cited from each brief's DoD; open questions they raised are collected in `70_MVP_INDEX.md` §5 and answered by Stack when each phase's PM session freezes its Contract | keeps the briefs the single place a worker reads, and keeps Stack's answers dated |
| 2026-09-15 | Phase 10 migration range is **046–059**: 10a used 046–051 plus 052–056 for its review round, 057–058 are slack, **059 is held for V-1's reconciliation** and 10a never takes it | 041–045 were live from Phase 9's fix rounds by the time 10a started, so the 041 figure in the session prompt was stale; Stack: "go with what works". Phase 9 needed five fix-round numbers, hence the slack |
| 2026-09-15 | Parallel PM sessions never branch or commit in the shared checkout `C:/Users/stack/projects/bb2dash`; each phase branch is its **own worktree** (`bb2dash-wt-<phase>`) and the brief is edited there | the Phase 10a and Phase 11 PM sessions edited two briefs in the same `main` working tree within 25 seconds of each other; a `checkout -b` or `add -A` in either would have carried the other's uncommitted file |
| 2026-09-15 | Gradebook mirror `bb_gradebook` is **append-per-run**, keyed `(run_id, course_id, column_id)`, reading `effectiveScore` (never `score`, null on every column); each row is classified `column_kind ∈ item / attendance / total / calc_other / letter`, a total being an `isCalc` column whose `formula.isTotalCalculation` is true or whose name matches 034's total regex; every `v_*` view reads only runs with a real `sync_runs` row | R-10; checked against the 9/14 crawl (45 columns, one total: IST.323 5/104, `Final Letter Grade` is a non-calc override column with no score). Unregistered runs are quarantined by 035/039 and must not leak into a score either |
| 2026-09-15 | An attendance column renders **among the item rows only when its linked assignment has a `component_id`** (`v_gradebook_latest.counts_toward_grade`); otherwise it sits in a collapsed "Attendance and bookkeeping columns" group with the letter and non-total calculated columns. Nothing is summed in 10a | Stack: collapsed, "however attendance may be a grade … it needs to show up correctly where it counts". Whether it counts is V-1's data (`assignments.component_id`), so the screen follows V-1's links instead of a client-side list; today every attendance row is unlinked and starts in the group |
| 2026-09-15 | `/grades` is grouped **by course** (Home card order); the popout shows **submission status, attempts and files, never a score**; Blackboard's attempt `receipt` is stored in `bb_attempts` but **not rendered**; a row with feedback and no score shows Blackboard's status verbatim (no "returned, ungraded" state); a staged file whose sha256 matches a pulled-back copy **stays listed** with a "matches" chip | Stack's answers 1–4 and 6 of 2026-09-15; the receipt "tells him nothing he acts on", feedback-without-score is an edge case outside the project's purpose |
| 2026-09-15 | **Reversal adopted (Requirements v2 §5): new Blackboard endpoints** — the crawler adds `…/gradebook/columns/{col}/attempts` and `…/gradebook/attempts/{id}/files`, records per-column HTTP status instead of throwing, and carries a `keys` probe on the first raw attempt; every `kind = 'course'` payload gains `crawler.version = 3` | R-17; the exact attempt key names cannot be verified without Stack's MFA session, so the probe settles them from the first live crawl the way `authorSource` settled the announcement author in Phase 9 |
| 2026-09-15 | Submission bytes are pulled by a new **bb-sync step 4b** in the logged-in tab (Playwright download → sha256 → Storage at `bb_file_relpath` → mirror → row update); `stage_attempts` only catalogues the files (`bucket = 'my_submissions'`, `classified_by = 'blackboard'`, `attempt_id`). The `classifier` enum gains `'blackboard'` (§6.2 #2 adopted); `bb_files.source_url` becomes nullable with a check that only `classified_by = 'stack'` rows may lack one | the crawler runs in a browser tab and cannot fetch cross-origin bytes (bbcswebdav lesson); the pull already works with Playwright (9/14 sync). A staged upload has no Blackboard URL, so `not null` had to go for exactly that row class |
| 2026-09-15 | `bb_files` anon INSERT is tightened to refuse `bucket = 'my_submissions'` and any `classified_by` of `stack` or `blackboard`; the Storage `bb_files_anon_insert` policy (bytes only) is **left as is** | an anonymous `bb_files` row would appear in Materials as one of Stack's submissions; an anonymous Storage object with no catalogue row is invisible to every screen, and the pull step uploads with the publishable key — tightening it would have needed the owner's JWT inside the skill |
| 2026-09-15 | The phase's only real crawl is **step (0) of Stack's acceptance script**, run with the new crawler before merge; workers build and test on fixtures cut from the 9/14 payloads plus a synthetic attempts payload in the frozen shape | every Blackboard login needs Stack's Duo push; this is a one-user application (Stack may teach a few others the sync method). | 2026-09-27 | **Phase 15: the SQL suite runs through `scripts/db-test.mjs` over node-postgres as the dedicated `db_test_runner` role (migration 100), with the DSN in the gitignored `.env.local` as `BB2DASH_TEST_DB_URL`** (B-42's default, batch item 42). Session pooler, `aws-0-us-east-1.pooler.supabase.com:5432`; the transaction pooler on 6543 is refused by the runner. The password was generated on Stack's laptop, set in one unsaved dashboard SQL tab, and never passed through a chat. pgTAP is not adopted. **The DSN needs `?uselibpqcompat=true&sslmode=require`**: `sslmode=require` alone fails on this laptop, because pg 8.23 aliases `require` to `verify-full` and the pooler chains to a private root (`self-signed certificate in certificate chain`). With the flag, libpq's own `require` semantics apply — encrypted, chain not verified. **What "least privilege" means here, precisely** (recorded 2026-09-28 after the security gate, because the phrase alone overstates it): the role's enumerated grants are least-privilege against *accident*, and the `anon` / `authenticated` memberships held with `inherit false` are what make the suite's "anon cannot read X" assertions mean anything. They are **not** a containment boundary against whoever holds the DSN — membership permits `set role authenticated`, `auth.uid()` reads `request.jwt.claims`, and any session can `set_config` that, so a DSN holder satisfies the owner-scoped RLS policies and reaches what `authenticated` holds. That follows from owner-by-email RLS over a direct connection (2026-09-10), not from this phase. The guard block's real work is therefore its negative list: no `service_role` or `postgres` membership, no usage on `vault`, `storage`, `auth` or `cron`, and neither Vault RPC executable, so the Google secrets stay out of reach. Treat the DSN as an owner-level credential for data and a strictly limited one for secrets. | His CLAUDE.md makes passing tests mandatory, and the suite had never run as a set; MCP SQL cannot be scripted. Decision #8 (2026-09-16) allows one gitignored secrets file, and a least-privilege DSN is narrower than a Management API token. The transaction pooler breaks the eight `set local role` switches. pgTAP would expose about fifty functions on the PostgREST surface and wants the local stack D-20 declines. The weaker-but-standard TLS setting is what `psql`'s `require` does and what research 92 assumed; the stronger `verify-full` with `sslrootcert` stays available and needs the CA from the dashboard, since Supabase's public CA download now 404s. |

Reverses Phase 12's 2026-09-16 call "launch at login: out" for the shell only. | 2026-09-29 | **The byte fetch no longer uses a browser download.** `ingest/fetch_signed.mjs` walks the `bbcswebdav` redirect chain to its signed CDN URL one hop at a time (injected `get`, `maxRedirects: 0`), bounded at 3 hops, and the final host must sit on `.content.blackboardcdn.com` checked at a domain boundary. `pull_files.mjs --fetch` then downloads the signed URL with a plain fetch, because a signed URL carries its own authorisation. Stopping the run on a dead session replaces one error per remaining file with one line |

| 2026-10-04 | **The desktop's update helper is handed off in two stages, and the app quits only after the helper's `swap-pending` marker names the build.** `update-os.ts` runs `update-now.ps1 -Detach` as a hidden, non-detached child (30 s bound, exit 0 required); that stage starts the real helper through `Start-Process -WindowStyle Hidden` (`Invoke-HelperHandoff`; `New-HelperHandoffArgumentList` quotes whitespace, doubles trailing backslashes, refuses `"`) and exits; the app then polls the marker for up to 20 s. A marker whose write time has not changed since before the press is a leftover and never counts; a fresh marker naming another build makes the helper refuse; a helper that finds the app still running after its wait starts nothing and says so; a transient marker read error counts as waiting until the deadline. | 2026-10-07 | **Phase 21 · the review round's rulings on the runner: the tool rule fails closed by count, a finished answer is retried for 110 s, and a lost reply from begin closes the request.** (1) A tool result that is not an error needs an allow from the gate (a PreToolUse hook answer with exit 0) for that tool name: results are counted per tool name and may never exceed the allows seen, and one that does ends the turn as `cli_error`. An error result with no hook answer (the CLI's own "No such tool available") is stored `ok: false` and the turn goes on; `EndConversation` is the one exemption; a gate exit other than 0 or 2 still ends the turn. (2) `workspace_finish()` is retried with backoff (1 s doubling, capped at 15 s) for up to 110 s (`FINISH_RETRY_MS`) before the answer is given up and logged; a 22023 means the request is already closed and is not retried. A turn is killed at 8 minutes, so the last try lands inside the database's 10-minute claim. (3) A `workspace_begin()` failure means "nothing to close" only when it is a 22023 on the first try. Any other failure is retried, and a 22023 that follows a failure of another kind (the first call may have landed and only its reply been lost) closes the request as `failed` / `cli_error`. Also: after its result line the CLI gets 10 s to exit and the result is kept (`RESULT_EXIT_GRACE_MS`), so a finished answer is never stored as `timeout`. | `/code-review main high`, first run on 2026-10-06: fourteen findings, two of them HIGH. CR-1 (HIGH): any tool result with no hook answer ended the turn, including errors the CLI writes itself without running the tool. CR-3 (HIGH): finish was tried three times a second apart and then abandoned, so a database blip at the end of a turn discarded a finished answer. CR-2: every begin failure was read as "no longer claimed", which left the request claimed for ten minutes. CR-6: hook answers were paired to calls by arrival order. PM rulings V1 and X1 (2026-10-06) and Y1 (2026-10-07) in 102a; the first window was 170 s, and 110 s is the one that fits under the claim. One corner is kept and recorded: the 110 s count from the finish's first try, so with a dead connection at the kill the last try can land about 8 s after the claim; with one runner nothing sweeps the request meanwhile, and a late finish is refused with 22023 (STATUS, Known issues). |
| 2026-10-07 | **Phase 21 · the firewall's allowlist is by address, and on port 443 code inside the container can walk around it through Cloudflare's shared addresses: recorded and deferred, put to Stack, not accepted on his behalf.** The Workspace container may reach three names only, and the firewall keeps that rule by address and port. The project's Supabase host sits on addresses Cloudflare shares among its customers, so a program running as `node` inside the container can reach any other Cloudflare-hosted site by dialling an allowed address with another server name: the independent check got a 200 from `example.com` through `104.18.38.10:443` (through Anthropic's address the same request gets 403). The firewall's own end check, "example.com refused", is true for example.com's own address only. The assistant has no tool that can do this (tools are off; four read-only MCP tools); it needs code running as `node`, which can already read the four secrets. Closing it means a name-checking proxy in front of port 443. Nothing is changed in this phase; STATUS lists it under the deferred hardening, with two notes from the same check: Docker's resolver answers any name for `node`, so DNS is a channel out, and `console.anthropic.com` and `claude.ai` share `api.anthropic.com`'s address and connect. | Found on 2026-10-07 by the independent check of the docker step (tasks 12 and 13; 102a), as a design call and not a defect of the build. The first `/security-review` request had named it in the abstract ("the allowlist is by address and both allowed hosts serve other tenants") and that review raised no finding on it; this is the concrete proof. Whether v1 lives with it or the proxy is built is Stack's call, put to him in the hand-off. Until he answers it is an open question, not an accepted risk. |

| 2026-10-07 | **Phase 21 · the range review's rulings on the runner (Z1): a result read after the runner's own abort is not a reported result, durations run on a monotonic clock, a statement the database refuses is not retried, and a secret holding a NUL is refused.** This amends the 2026-10-07 row above on the review round's runner rulings. (1) When the runner has aborted a turn (the 8-minute limit, a Stop, a shutdown), a `result` line the provider reads afterwards does not make the turn `done` and does not replace the abort's code. A result read before the abort still wins, as before. (2) The runner measures its durations on a monotonic clock: the finish and begin retry window, the watchdog hold and the turn limit, and with them the watchdog's own 180 s, the 2 s Stop poll and the stored duration. Timestamps that are written or logged stay wall-clock. (3) A failure that is the statement's own (the data, integrity and syntax classes 22, 23 and 42; 22023 keeps its own meaning; 57014 and every connection failure are still retried) ends the tries at once, and NUL characters are stripped from every string inside `tool_calls`. A finish refused that way is followed by one minimal close of the same request (`failed`, `cli_error`, no content, no tool calls), so the request does not sit claimed until the 10-minute sweep. (4) A secret file holding a NUL is refused with a configuration error that names the file and no part of its value: the DSN at the runner's start, the sign-in token where it is read, before each CLI start (that turn ends `sign_in_expired`, and a replaced token file still needs no restart). | 2026-10-07 | **Acceptance run · a phase's acceptance walk is carried out by a Claude session in a disposable sandbox container, the host does the Docker steps and checks the facts itself, and a fully green run counts as acceptance.** `just accept <phase>` in bb2dash-stack. Only the tester is new each run; it tests the live site against whichever Workspace runner is up. The sandbox holds the Claude sign-in token and nothing else: no Docker access, no database login, no app password, its own network and a short firewall list. The host script runs the Docker steps from a fixed list (a phase's pack can name an action, never supply a command), signs the sandbox in and out of the app, and proves the hard facts from the database, read-only, with the test login that stays on Stack's laptop. Green needs both a passing report from the sandbox and a passing proof from the host for every automated step; "unsure", skipped or missing is not green. | 2026-10-08 | **Acceptance run · the review round: nothing the sandbox says or writes is enough by itself to make a step green, and no way of ending a run leaves the machine worse than it found it (PM rulings).** Because a green run counts as acceptance, `/code-review` and `/security-review` on both PRs were held to that one rule. What follows from it: (1) every proof is tied to the run (the host's own start time), to the step's question (its md5, held to the browser test's own string by a test) and to the step before it, so one request cannot stand for two steps; a stop counts only when the runner had taken the question, and the question asked while the service was stopped must have waited at least 15 s. (2) The proofs script sends every statement by the extended protocol in a read-only transaction, so the server itself refuses a second statement or a write; it returns only values of an allowed shape, never text. (3) The host signs the sandbox's app session out with its own copy of the token, taken before the stage; a session that could not be ended makes the stage red. (4) Declared evidence must have content, and the session's values are searched for in everything a stage leaves. (5) Ctrl+C, a kill or a closed terminal end in the same cleanup; the next run first puts right what a cut-off run left; one run at a time; a runner that could not be put back ends the run with exit 4 and one loud line. (6) A run started while a sync or Inbox apply is open stops at once as blocked, before it stops a container or asks a question. (7) Only the production site is accepted as the site under test, and a run whose host scripts come from another checkout than the one compose builds from never counts. **Known and left as they are:** what the page showed is the sandbox's word (the browser tests run inside it), and step 2 has no host proof: every report names the steps that rest on the sandbox alone. A run against a branch preview is designed and not built.
